London Financial Coup

Between 1664 and 1694, London experienced the most concentrated destabilization sequence in its history — a thirty-year arc transforming the last independent financial center in Europe into headquarters of a centralized banking system that would eventually govern the world.

This was not coincidence. It was a coordinated campaign:

• 1664–65: A great comet appeared for over 80 days, interpreted as divine judgment

• 1664–66: The Great Plague killed 15% of London's population, devastating guild and goldsmith communities

• Sept 2–6, 1666: The Great Fire destroyed 13,200 houses, 87 parish churches, the Guildhall, St. Paul's Cathedral, and the Royal Exchange — the physical infrastructure of the old financial order

• 1666: The Cestui Que Vie Act — a legal mechanism to declare absent persons dead and their estates managed by others

• 1667: The Dutch burned the English fleet — military humiliation leading to Clarendon's fall

• 1668–74: Charles II ruled through the Cabal — five non-Anglican ministers, two secret Catholics, in a Protestant nation

• 1672: The Stop of the Exchequer — Charles suspended all payments, ruining independent goldsmiths and destroying 10,000 families

• 1694: The Bank of England was founded on a Mithras temple — centralizing all currency under a joint-stock corporation answerable to shareholders, not citizens. Charles II implemented William Paterson's plan: the Governor and Company of the Bank of England was chartered as a joint-stock company with limited liability; 1,268 investors raised £1.2 million in 11 days for 8% interest. The Bank was established in an old Roman temple on Walbrook Street where Mithras, god of contracts, was worshipped.

Britannica confirms the sequence was recognized at the time:

"The Great Plague of London (1664–66) and the Great Fire of London (1666) were interpreted as divine judgments against a sinful nation. These catastrophes were compounded when the Dutch burned a large portion of the English fleet in 1667, which led to the dismissal and exile of Clarendon. The crown's debts led to the Stop of the Exchequer (1672), by which Charles suspended payment of his bills." — Britannica, "The Later Stuarts"

The Goldsmiths: The Real Target

The London goldsmiths were not merely wealthy merchants. They were the last independent financial power in Europe — inheritors of the old Imperial treasury infrastructure that Fomenko identifies as moved to England by the Reformers (Ch.7 §18). Their ability to independently appraise gold and determine goods' relative value made them a direct threat to royal currency. As long as independent valuation existed, no centralized monetary authority could achieve total control.

The gold ended up in London through Imperial trade flows and deliberate transfer of the Horde's treasury. The goldsmiths managed it independently as a professional guild. But the Pyramidal Empire needed to seize control to install the new centralized system. The 1664–1694 sequence was the mechanism.

The Apocalyptic Context

The entire sequence was framed by an apocalyptic prophecy: 1666 was read as "the 666th year of our Lord" — the number of the beast from Revelation. London's leadership was told of impending divine judgment. When each catastrophe arrived — comet, plague, fire — it confirmed the prophecy, silencing political will of those who might have resisted.

Sources

• Britannica, "The Later Stuarts"

• A.T. Fomenko, How It Was In Reality, Ch.7 §18, Ch.9 §3

• Cestui Que Vie Act 1666, Chapter 11, 18 & 19 Cha. 2

Author's Theory: This interpretation is my own reconstruction. No online sources correlating these events in this sequence have been found.

The Year of Our Lord 666

The year 1666 was a weapon. Written as ι666, it read as "in the 666th year of the Lord," invoking the Number of the Beast. For a population steeped in apocalyptic Christianity, this date carried existential weight.

During the first half of the seventeenth century some extravagant notions of the near approach of the Messianic time, and more especially of the redemption of the Jews and their return to Jerusalem, were set forth by Christian writers and entertained by Jews and Christians alike. The so-called apocalyptic year was assigned by Christian authors to the year 1666.
JewishEncyclopedia.com

I believe London's leadership was told of an impending apocalypse for September 1666 — the same technique used in 1492 with prophetic letters.

London was not alone. Across the Jewish diaspora — Smyrna to Amsterdam, Aleppo to Hamburg — tens of thousands believed 1666 would bring the end of history. Jewish communities across three continents entered mass mania — abandoning livelihoods, preparing for return to the Holy Land.

Both prophecies collapsed in September 1666. London burned to the ground (13,200 houses, 87 churches, St. Paul's Cathedral). Simultaneously, the Ottoman Sultan arrested Zevi and forced his conversion to Islam. Two religious populations spanning Europe, North Africa, and the Middle East had been convinced — and both shattered in the same month.

The Sequence of Belief and Doubt (London)

11.04.02-1666-ce-london-burns-to-the-ground-sept-2-6-1666.md
========================================================================

London Burns to the Ground (Sept 2-6, 1666)

From 2 to 6 September 1666 the Great Fire of London burned through the walled city. It followed years in which bubonic plague had dominated English life; plague was finally loosening its hold on the country when the fire broke. Roughly 13,200 houses, eighty-seven parish churches, the Royal Exchange, Guildhall, and St Paul’s Cathedral—most of it medieval fabric—were destroyed; loss was estimated at about £10 million. Relations between the City and the Crown were already strained. During the English Civil War (1642–1651) London had been a centre of republican sentiment, and unrest in the early 1660s still complicated Charles II’s position. Many City magistrates had lived through the war and associated Charles I’s centralizing policies with the breakdown that followed; when the fire broke out they declined the king’s offers of troops and supplies, because admitting royal soldiers into London—even in an emergency—was widely regarded as politically hazardous. By the time Charles took a directing role from the Lord Mayor, the blaze had spread beyond ready control.

This chronology holds that the fire’s timing did more than clear streets and warehouses: it ended a nationwide plague emergency as the main public preoccupation at a moment when corrupt Vatican canon law and fiduciary innovation could advance with little open opposition—benefiting Deep State interests and allied secret societies through presumptions that native sovereigns were dead at sea, stripping sovereignty and estate from birth (via birth certificate), the hidden cestui que vie trust machinery and related accounts, the reduction of standing from Master to Mr., and wider override of older law through legal entrapment.

On the longer arc, 1666 is a climax of the Deep State—the moment that continuity was most nakedly deep. The country had been conditioned by plague and millennial expectation to interpret ruin as judgment and the return of God or Jesus to set the world right. What advanced instead was legal-fiduciary settlement: cestui que vie, native sovereigns “dead at sea,” standing reduced from Master to Mr.—so that authority revealed itself as the Beast, rule that wore sacred language while serving invisible accounts and secret allegiance. Only after that demonstration did the same continuity feel secure enough, in the centuries that followed, to resurface with overt pyramidal architecture and iconography worldwide. Beneath that visibility, this Inland Empire still prefers the deep mode—penetration of every human institution and systematic suppression of knowledge.

London's Rebuilding and New Financial Order

Political aftermath is as significant as fire itself. When rebuilding plan was implemented, medieval guildhall structure and warren of organic property rights defining old City was replaced with regularized street grid — conveniently amenable to new financial architecture being planned. The Cestui Que Vie Act of 1666, passed same year as fire, created legal presumption that any person absent from estate for seven years could be declared legally dead, with estate administered by others. Long-term implications for property law, inheritance, and formation of financial trusts governing Western institutional life have never been fully traced.

NC Context: England as New Financial Metropoly

In Fomenko's reconstruction (Ch.7 §18), England's rise to global financial dominance was not organic — it was funded by stolen treasury of Hordian Empire, moved to British Isles specifically because geographically remote from Russia-Horde and any potential imperial restoration. Fire of London served this project by destroying physical evidence of old order (87 churches, medieval Guildhall, St. Paul's Cathedral, thousands of private records) and forcing complete legal and financial reset. Royal Exchange, one of few institutions that might have anchored independent financial system, was among structures destroyed.

That Charles II — himself restored only six years earlier — attempted to insert royal troops into City during crisis and was blocked by City Magistrates illustrates how thoroughly London's financial elite had consolidated their independence from Crown. Within twenty-eight years, Bank of England would formalize that independence into institutional law.

Open speculation

Is it possible that science tropes like H. G. Wells’s The Crystal Egg (1897: crystal as distant relay / window) could metaphorically or literally point to earlier secret coordination of undisclosed technology to create mass expectation before the 1666 climax, or whether other drivers alone (fire, plague, law, finance, Fomenko’s Horde-treasury thesis, apocalyptic numerology, mercantile networks) suffice to explain England’s sudden reorganization and later imperial shape. See /history/chronology/investigations/wells-crystal-egg-1666-beast-speculation.md. Status: sensational placeholder; not chronology.

11.04.03-the-goldsmiths-and-the-death-of-independent-valuation.md
========================================================================

The Goldsmiths and the Death of Independent Valuation

Author's Theory: The interpretation below is my own reconstruction. The argument that destroying the goldsmith class represented the death of independent economic valuation — and the origin of modern poverty — is not found in any single source. If you encounter supporting or contradicting evidence, please contribute to the investigation.

More Than Money-Lenders

The goldsmiths were not merely wealthy merchants. They were a threat to the royal currency itself. A goldsmith could appraise gold's exact purity, value a cargo against gold weight, examine land or buildings and assign values rooted in physical reality. As long as they could do this, they could declare things more or less valuable than the Crown decreed. Two competing valuation systems cannot coexist — one must destroy the other.

The Imperial Inheritance

Per Fomenko (Ch.7 §18): "It is possible that the Reformers moved the stolen treasury of the Empire to England... Having seized the vast treasures of the Horde, the English rebels acquired influence and created the 'English Empire'."

The gold London's smiths appraised was residue of the Hordian Imperial treasury. The goldsmiths were inheritors of the old financial infrastructure — custodians of the pre-centralized system who had to be crushed to install the new one.

The System That Replaced Them

The Bank of England, founded in 1694 on the site of a Mithras temple — the Roman god of contracts — was the replacement of independent valuation with institutional decree. Where the goldsmith told you what things were actually worth, the Bank told you what things were declared worth — measurement versus proclamation. Its authority derived not from gold weight but Crown power. Other banks were prohibited from issuing banknotes — making independent valuation illegal.

The Birth of Poverty

This switch started real-world poverty never before seen on Earth. Before the Bank, wealth could be independently verified — gold is gold, measurable fact. Poverty was local and addressable. After the Bank, wealth became a declaration — a number on a ledger maintained by shareholders, not citizens. You could stand on gold-rich land, grow food, produce goods of measurable value, and still be "poor" because the ledger said so. Modern poverty is not scarcity but exclusion from an arbitrary accounting system.

The Sequence of Destruction

• 1660: Charles II restored — subject to immediate financial pressures

• 1660: Parliament caps interest at 6%

• 1662: Hearth taxes — biannual audits of every household

• 1664–66: Comet, Plague, Fire — physical destruction of goldsmith community

• 1667–72: Charles borrows from goldsmiths at rates exceeding the 6% cap

• 1672: Great Stop of the Exchequer — Charles suspends all payments, destroying the goldsmiths

• 1694: Bank of England founded — monopoly on banknotes, independent valuation made illegal

From Acronym to Accusation

"Cabal" entered English as a term for any secretive political faction operating behind the scenes. If such an entity had ended, it would have been defeated and exposed at some point in three centuries. The 19th–21st century tools of surveillance and communication are powerful enough to expose any purely human organization at scale. But that never happened.

Nobody knows who runs the so-called Cabal. The closest we get are institutional names — the Bank for International Settlements, the Federal Reserve, the City of London Corporation, the Vatican Bank — but these are instruments, not controllers. An organization maintaining coherent strategy across four centuries, surviving every revolution, adapting to every technology, and never decisively exposed either represents an unprecedented achievement in human organizational theory, or something else entirely.

The Cabal and the Financial Coup

Within the 1664–1694 financial coup, the Cabal governed England during the transition between destruction of the old financial order (the goldsmiths) and installation of the new (the Bank of England). Clarendon represented the last link to older, more accountable governance. His removal ensured no powerful minister could resist the financial restructuring. The Stop of the Exchequer in 1672 — destroying the goldsmiths — occurred on the Cabal's watch.

British Empire as Deep State Instrument

On the Deep State's formation and role, see Humanity cast out and Deep State, Census.

Author's Theory: This analysis is my own interpretation. No online sources correlating British Empire behavior across all these case studies have been found.

The Thesis

The British Empire (1583–1997) was not a conventional empire. Unlike Rome, the Mongols, the Horde, or the Ottomans, Britain did not want to retain its colonies. It conquered territory, divided populations along racial/ethnic markers, armed both sides with advanced weapons, established financial control — then left at the moment calculated to produce maximum chaos.

This strategy did not benefit Britain and could only come from a hypothetical Pyramidal Empire.

The Method

• Arrive — establish military/commercial presence via missionary activity

• Map and divide — ethnographic surveys identifying every fault line

• Arm both sides — supply modern weapons to competing factions

• Establish financial control — centralized currency, debt dependencies

• Leave at the right moment — withdraw when armed populations will fight each other

• "Order from chaos" — maintain invisible control through financial ties over divided, indebted successor states

Case studies: India/Pakistan (Partition, 15M displaced, nuclear standoff); Israel (Balfour + armed both sides → 1948 war); Africa (Berlin Conference borders, Hutu-Tutsi); Japan/China (Opium Wars, Meiji); Hong Kong (incompatible institutions). Full detail: British Empire Case Studies. The slave trade and colonization that accompanied empire were, under the Horde-Fracture thesis, direct results of the breakup of the Rus-Horde — splinter states competing for labour and resources.

Who Benefited?

The British people never benefited. Workers lived in Dickensian poverty while the Empire extracted planetary wealth. The NHS wasn't established until 1948 — the same year India was partitioned. The City of London — a one-square-mile corporation with its own jurisdiction, police, and governance — is not governed by the UK government. It has its own parliamentary representative (the Remembrancer). This is a private institution located in Britain.

The Language Question

Britain never spoke Latin. Yet its institutional framework operates in Latin: habeas corpus, Cestui Que Vie, Rex, lex, fiat. 11.05.01-jesuits-missions-convert-asians-to-buddhism.md

Jesuits Missions convert Asians to Buddhism

The 17th century was the century of maximum Jesuit ambition — and maximum resistance. Having established footholds in the Americas (15th century) and Africa/Middle East (16th century), the Society of Jesus turned to Asia.

Converting eastern Gnostic Christians into western Catholics proved impossible. Asian communities had detailed records of Christ — their first Emperor — spanning centuries after his death, with no record of him being a god's son. They rejected monotheism entirely. The Jesuits fell back on assimilating their saviour myth into a different popular character: The Buddha. Fomenko summarizes the strategy:

The idea is to improve the icon of Jesus by assimilating it to the Buddha… Buddha and Jesus have similar biographies, because they are the fruit of a common project.

Japan: Complete Rejection

The Tokugawa shogunate's expulsion of Christianity represents the most successful resistance to the Jesuit strategy anywhere. Christianity gained a foothold through Francis Xavier (arrived 1549); by the early 1600s about 300,000 had converted. Increasingly severe persecution culminated in the Shimabara Rebellion (1637–38) — an uprising of largely Christian peasants crushed with ~37,000 killed. Japan closed to Western contact under Sakoku policy until 1853.

Missionary deaths — the 26 Martyrs (1597), the Great Martyrdom of Nagasaki (1622) — became European propaganda, but Japan's institutional memory of its Hordian connections was strong enough to recognize and reject the Jesuit project entirely.

China: The Court Astronomers

In China, Jesuits achieved what they couldn't in Japan: court access. Matteo Ricci (1552–1610) won Imperial favour through astronomy and cartography. By the 17th century, Johann Adam Schall von Bell and Ferdinand Verbiest directed the Imperial Astronomical Bureau — literally controlling the Chinese calendar.

But the Chinese Rites controversy — whether Chinese Catholics could continue Confucian ancestor veneration — torpedoed the mission. In 1724, the Yongzheng Emperor banned Christian proselytization and expelled missionaries.

Southeast Asia and India

Roberto de Nobili (1577–1656) pioneered inculturation in India — dressing as a Brahmin, studying Sanskrit. The Philippines (under Spanish control) was the easiest target; Vietnam proved more resistant.

The Martyr Pattern

Across every Asian theatre, missionaries followed the same pattern: peaceful arrival, trust-building, conversion pressure, death at the hands of communities recognizing the existential threat, then death publicized as evidence of Asian "barbarism." Each martyrdom created moral grounds for military intervention. The missionaries took the punishment; the empire used their suffering as moral currency. See Jesuit Restoration.

Buddhism and the NC Framework

The NC observation that Buddhism and Christianity share biographical structures — miraculous births, temptation narratives, teaching careers, symbolic deaths — is not coincidence but design. Both figures express the same Imperial religious archetype — the Christ-Andronicus figure of the 12th century — adapted for different provincial audiences.

Slave Trades and the Horde Fracture

The great slave trades of the 16th–19th centuries — transatlantic (African), Barbary (white/European), Arab trans-Saharan, and Indian Ocean — did not emerge in isolation. They coincide with and are, under the timeline's framework, a direct result of the fracture of the Rus-Horde. See Slave Trades: Evidence and Horde-Fracture Thesis for full scope and investigation.

The Thesis

When the Deep State broke up the Hordian Empire (early 17th c.), former vassal aristocratic families became independent sovereigns — Russia, Ottoman, Austria, France, Spain, England, Portugal, and the rest. These detached aristocracies had no loyalty to the old Imperial center. They competed for territory, labour, and resources. Slavery and colonization became the tools of that competition.

• Transatlantic trade: ~12.5 million Africans enslaved (15th–19th c.); Portuguese first voyage 1526; British, French, Dutch participation. Peak 17th–18th centuries — immediately after the fracture.

• Barbary slave trade: 1–1.25 million Europeans enslaved by Moorish corsairs (16th–18th c.); Italians, Spanish, Irish, British; raids as far as Iceland.

• Who benefited: Not European populations. Research suggests colonialism's costs often exceeded benefits for the colonizing nations. The British Empire thesis: the strategy did not benefit Britain — it could only serve a cabal of financial/military interests.

The Humanitarian Contrast

The old Serbian/Slavic (Rus-Horde) empire is routinely depicted in mainstream historiography as "Mongol barbarism." Under the Horde-Fracture thesis, the unified empire was far more humanitarian than the splinter states that replaced it. The splinter empires and detached aristocracies engaged in the worst crimes in human history for centuries — mass enslavement, genocide, extractive colonialism — because they had no Imperial constraint and every motive to maximize short-term extraction.

Missionary-Colonial Link

Jesuits reached Africa in 1548; colonial administrators followed missionary stations. The missionary → commerce → administration pattern established the infrastructure for the transatlantic trade. The timeline's divide-and-conquer playbook — arrive, map and divide, arm both sides, establish financial control, leave — applies to the slave trade as much as to decolonization.

18th Century: MudFlood, Pugachev & the Jesuit Order

The 18th–19th centuries span MudFlood, Pugachev, Mars catastrophe, and the Trail of Tears campaign. By the early 18th century, the Jesuit Society of Jesus had operated as a transnational covert organization for nearly two centuries — infiltrating courts, universities, and intelligence channels across every civilization. With Hordian resistance neutralized by the Romanovs and Moscow Tartary not yet organized for counter-attack, the Society moved into its openly dominating phase.

The treaties of London (1718) and Vienna (1725) formalized this new order: coordinated European colonial project dividing the non-European world between Western powers, operating under Jesuit theological and banking architecture.

the treaties of London - of 1718 - and of Vienna - of 1725 - in which Europe conquers half the world.
— Per the Marfull chronological framework

The Jesuit Educational Empire

By the 18th century Jesuits operated over 800 universities, seminaries, and colleges across Europe, the Americas, and Asia. This was their core strategy: control the education of the ruling class and you control the next generation's worldview. Students became the Society's eyes and ears in every court, parliament, and colonial administration. The Jesuits didn't need spies; they had alumni.

The 1773 Suppression: When the Tool Outgrew the Toolmaker

On July 21, 1773, Pope Clement XIV issued Dominus ac Redemptor, dissolving the Society worldwide. The suppression was demanded by Bourbon monarchies of France, Spain, Portugal, and Naples — the same powers the Jesuits had helped bring to dominance. Stated reason: too politically powerful, too financially independent. Real reason: the tool had outgrown the toolmaker.

The Jesuits had accumulated enormous wealth — plantations, trading operations, real estate — with intelligence channels rivalling nation-states. They answered to the Superior General, not local monarchs. They acted independently: the Guaraní Reductions in Paraguay were effectively an autonomous Jesuit state.

In Portugal (1759), Jesuits were imprisoned or deported. In France (1764), property confiscated. In Spain (1767), over 5,000 expelled overnight. Approximately 23,000 Jesuits worldwide were affected.

The Russian Exception

Catherine the Great refused to publish the papal brief, allowing Jesuits to operate in the Russian Empire — particularly Belarus. The order created to help dismantle the Hordian order found sanctuary in its successor state, because the Romanovs recognized Jesuit administrative value.

This Russian haven kept the Society alive during forty-one years of dissolution and became the nucleus from which it was restored in 1814.

The Paradox

The 18th century reveals the central Jesuit paradox: an organization simultaneously serving imperial interests and destroyed by those interests when it became too effective.

The Jesuits genuinely educated millions. Missionaries genuinely documented indigenous cultures with care no Protestant matched. Individual Jesuits genuinely advocated for indigenous communities. But the system they served never intended to produce an autonomous conscience-bearing organization — it intended obedient operatives.

When Jesuits developed independent judgment — questioning the slave trade, defending indigenous land rights, accumulating wealth outside royal control — the system that created them destroyed them. Temporarily. The pattern continued.

The Rebellion of Pugachev

In 1774 Pugachev's rebellion peaked. Claiming to be Peter III and direct descendant of Christ, Pugachev was positioned to reclaim the entire Tartarian Empire and overthrow the foreign Romanovs. Had he succeeded, Russia could have returned to former glory, re-imposing a Second Inquisition that would have erased the Reformation, state monotheism, slavery, and usury.

Pugachev was a giant human, as were much of his army. He was Peter III — actual heir to the Russian throne, not an impostor as Romanov historians claim. He led both giants and smaller humans against Romanov authority. This was the third major war between humans and giants. The first was the 12th-century Crusade. The second was the Battle of Kulikovo (1380), where cannon technology defeated the giants. By Pugachev's time, giants had their own cannons and had mastered guerrilla warfare. His rebellion threatened every military-religious order worldwide.

Moscow Tartary: The War's True Stakes

At the time (1773–1775), Moscow Tartary still existed as a functional sovereign state — labeled the largest country in the world in the 1771 Encyclopaedia Britannica. Its capital was Tobolsk (Biblical Tubal); its territory spanned from the Volga across the Urals, Siberia, Alaska, and North America west of the Atlantic settlements.

The "rebellion" was not a peasant uprising — it was war between two Russian states. The Tobolsk czar launched an offensive against Romanov Russia from the east, simultaneously with the Ottoman Empire attacking from the south. Fomenko writes:

"The conflict between Moscow Tartary and Romanov Russia ended in the second half of the XVIII century with the famous, allegedly 'peasant', war against 'Pugachev'. The Romanovs succeeded in agreeing peace separately with Turkey and defeating the Great Tartary."
— Fomenko, Ch.8 §1

Suvorov Defeated Pugachev — Concealed

Official Russian history credits victory to Lieutenant-Colonel Mikhailson. Fomenko demonstrates it was Alexander Suvorov — the century's greatest Russian commander — who personally led the decisive campaign. Concealment was deliberate: the Romanovs needed to present the conflict as a minor disturbance, not a massive campaign requiring their finest general.

Suvorov's title "Count of Rymnik" (supposedly for an 1789 battle in Romania) traces to the real battle on the Yaik/Rymnik River against Pugachev in 1774. A Romanian river was later renamed "Rymnik" to provide cover:

"It turns out that A.V.Suvorov's distinguished title of 'Count of Rymnik' is not related to the geographical name of 'Rymnik' in Romania… but to the old name of the Yaik River…"
— Fomenko, Ch.8 §3

USA and the Partition of Moscow Tartary

The American Declaration of Independence (1776) relative to Pugachev's defeat (1775) is not coincidental. The American colonies fought the War of Independence in the same years the last Hordian state collapsed.

"'The War of independence' in North America was the struggle with the weakening Russian Horde. The Romanovs attacked the Horde from the West — and from the East in America it was attacked by the Americans 'fighting for independence'."
— Fomenko, Ch.8 §1

With Pugachev defeated, the Romanovs and the new United States divided Moscow Tartary: Russia took the Urals, Siberia, and Far East; Americans moved west. Alaska — the last Hordian territory in America — was transferred to the USA only in 1867. Hollywood's mythology of heroic frontiersmen versus savage Indians is the American cover story for the military conquest of the world's once-largest state.

The MudFlood & World Cataclysm

The MudFlood Energetic Event (MFEE) describes a planet-scale calamity that struck Earth in the late 18th century, abruptly and permanently raising ground levels around existing cathedrals, pyramids, and monuments — burying entire civilizations in mud. Photographs, excavation reports, and comparative maps all document cities buried without satisfactory mainstream explanation: no tilting from liquefaction, no debris consistent with hydraulic drilling, no gradual accumulation. The event appears simultaneous and worldwide.

1774 CE — mysterious carbon-14 spike

The 774–775 CE carbon-14 spike (tree rings) — 20× normal variation, global — corrects to 1774 under phantom-time: 774 + 1000 = 1774. The Anglo-Saxon Chronicle for 774 records a "red crucifix" in the heavens and "wonderful serpents" — the same year as the Pugachev Rebellion. (Full C14 analysis: MudFlood Extended Evidence.)

The MFEE Hypothesis locates the trigger in the Pugachev Rebellion of 1773–1774 — the final war between the Royal Christian giants descended from Christ and the Romanov-backed forces. Prior to 1774, the breakaway human civilization on Mars is argued to have exercised control over Earth primarily through simulated deities — projecting godlike apparitions, omens, and interventions via remote-viewing and scrying technology (see Crystal Egg theory: two-way surveillance between Earth and Mars, encoded in Wells and later declassified). As long as humans believed an invisible divine hand guided their wars and harvests, the Martian operators could steer outcomes without leaving orbit. Remote control sufficed.

Pugachev's polytheistic gnostic kingdom posed a direct threat to that model. His movement contested the claim — enforced by priests and rulers — that a single monotheistic god was guiding humanity. People were revolting and no longer responding to the control systems implemented by the church: usury, fear of hell, liturgical obedience. The Romanovs did not enlist Martian intervention. If anything, they complained to God — or to the entities on the other side of the egg — that the faithful were in revolt. Through the same scrying channels that delivered divine appearances and omens, their appeals reached the Martian operators, who heard not prayer but a systems alert.

The Martians did everything they could to hide their identity. It was very unlikely that humans on Earth would figure out that the transmissions — visions, prophecies, judgments — came from another planet rather than from God or angels. When the operators deployed large-scale Directed Energy Weapons against Rus-Tartarian strongholds, they did so in a form that survivors would interpret as divine intervention: armies struck down, cities buried, the wrath of heaven. Annihilating Pugachev and his allies reinforced the monotheistic narrative. No one had to know who had pulled the trigger.

Partial evidence for this causal chain lies in the persistence of the belief. A large proportion of humans in the 19th, 20th, and 21st centuries absolutely hold that an invisible deity guides humanity — striking down armies, delivering plagues, and threatening yet another apocalypse. This conviction can be analyzed transactionally: a population subjected to planet-scale trauma, then told that an omnipotent god caused it, will often internalize the story. The result may resemble generational PTSD — a wound passed down through narratives of divine judgment, end-times prophecy, and chosen-people identity — lasting to the present.

The energy event tore up land and hurled matter miles into the air. Positive discharge pulled mountain ranges from the ground; negative discharge carved canyons identical to Mars lightning scarring. Instantaneous petrification of giants produced what archaeologists catalogued as dinosaur fossil graveyards. The cover-up: coordinated redaction of history, religion, science, language. World religions canonized — canon = authority and large gun — by the same force. The ability to erase the past gives control of the future.

12.04.00-instant-fossilization-electric.md

Instant Fossilization & Electric

Electric Universe researcher Peter Mungo Jupp, working with plasma physicists at the Thunderbolts Project, documented evidence that fossils were created instantaneously by catastrophic electrical discharge — not over millions of years. This project places the triggering event in 1774–1775 CE.

The Problem with Conventional Fossilization

Standard geology requires 10,000+ years of slow mineral replacement. This collapses against two facts: organic tissue decomposes within years, and the fossil record shows organisms at the precise moment of death — plesiosaur mid-labor, jellyfish mid-division, fish inside another fish (both petrified), ammonites with every detail intact, mammoths fused into calcium carbonate, trees still vertical and converted to solid rock.

The Electricity Evidence

E.R. Milton documented a downed power line in Alberta, Canada: every tree root touching the live wire was fossilized (converted to silica with fused sand crust). Wal Thornhill demonstrated the same at EU2017 with fulgurites. Water (H₂O) has 10 protons; calcium has 20. Russian studies confirm neutron propagation during lightning; adding neutrons to water molecules under massive current offers a pathway for transmuting H₂O to calcium carbonate — explaining aquatic creatures embedded in limestone without crushing or burning.

Plasmoids and the Carolina Bays

Rick Firestone (Berkeley) surveyed the Carolina Bays — tens of thousands of shallow elliptical depressions with no impact debris. Inside: nano-diamonds, iridium, helium-3, fullerenes, hollow spherules — the same fingerprint laboratory plasmoid experiments produce. Anthony Peratt (Los Alamos) independently confirmed that mythological accounts worldwide encode observations of giant plasma discharge events. The Bays were formed by plasmoids, not meteors.

The 1774–1775 Date

Every anchor converges on this window: the Pugachev Rebellion ends with directed energy against Rus-Tartarian strongholds; the MudFlood raises ground levels worldwide; the Carbon-14 spike of 774 (corrected for Phantom Time = 1774 CE); old maps show features that no longer exist (California as island, no Grand Canyon) — carved by the 1774–75 discharge. The Smithsonian was founded in 1846 and immediately began reclassifying giant remains.

The Dinosaur Cover Story

A cover story was needed for the sudden abundance of giant fossilized bones. Georges Cuvier recognized mass extinctions as catastrophic and sudden. Darwin's gradual model was seized upon: attributing bones to creatures 65–250 million years old erased the 1774 event, giant humanoid remains were reclassified as non-human (Gigantopithecus), and catastrophic evidence (Lichtenberg scars, nano-diamond layer, Carolina Bays) was attributed to causes safely buried in deep time.

What Petrification Records

Without the Scaligerian chronological frame, the fossil record is a snapshot of one catastrophic event. The Jurassic, Cretaceous, Permian "ages" represent ecological zones struck simultaneously: marine (ammonites), coastal (Lichtenberg surfaces), inland (megafauna bonebeds), forest (petrified trees). Layering in sedimentary rock is consistent with rapid hydraulic sorting under one electromagnetic event.

Peter Mungo Jupp at EU2017: "These fossils dramatically illustrate that these are not creatures which are disarticulated. They appear to have undergone no breakdown and decomposition. They are in very lifelike positions... It is an instantaneous thing."

The Mars Catastrophe

In the late 18th century, during the Pugachev Rebellion, humans on Mars faced an existential crisis. The rebellion threatened to dismantle the religious orders Martian factions controlled through the Rex Bellator Plan. In desperation, they deployed Directed Energy Weapons at Earth.

What they did not anticipate was the electrical rebound.

The Rebound Theory

In an Electric Universe, energy cannot be discharged at a target and simply absorbed. Planets are not solid spheres but hollow capacitors with multiple concentric shells.

When the Martians fired at Earth, energy entered the surface and propagated through its hollow shells. Each shell functions as a capacitor layer, charging to capacity before excess passes inward. The entire planet "charged up" like a massive electrical reservoir.

When Earth could absorb no more, the energy discharged outward through the poles. With Mars in opposition (closest approach), the path of least resistance was back to Mars. The weapon meant to subjugate Earth became the instrument of Mars's devastation. The melted city ruins photographed on Mars today bear witness: structures subjected to intense plasma discharge.

Open research question: If Mars was in opposition during the event, this might narrow down exactly when it happened. Opposition dates are calculable; cross-referencing them with MudFlood evidence, the 774/1774 CE carbon-14 spike, and anomalous celestial phenomena could pinpoint the catastrophe with precision.

Targeting the Telepaths

Why fire a weapon knowing it would devastate your own planet? One possibility: the operators wanted to destroy enemies on both planets simultaneously. The MudFlood Energetic Event would have been catastrophic for telepaths, who could not endure such intense electromagnetic disruption. A single strike could eliminate telepathic individuals on both planets at once.

The resulting power vacuum on Mars allowed non-telepathic factions to consolidate control — explaining how a single faction could wield extraordinary influence over 19th and 20th century Earth through secret societies and institutional infiltration.

Evidence of Recent Destruction

This theory is my own. No mainstream or alternative researcher has proposed Mars was devastated centuries ago rather than millions of years in the past. Yet the evidence aligns better with a recent catastrophe:

• Melted cities visible on the surface — Areas resembling melted urban structures with discernible buildings, consistent with rapid heating rather than ancient geological processes.

2. Mars in human memory — Under Saturnian Cosmology, Mars was intimately involved in the polar configuration during the Dark Ages. Its catastrophe should be in human memory.
3. The timing — The 1774 CE date correlates with the Pugachev Rebellion, the MudFlood, and the Carbon-14 spike of 774 CE.
4. War of the Worlds as disclosure — H.G. Wells's novel and the 1938 Orson Welles broadcast may be predictive programming — real events reframed as alien invasion.
5. The blood proves they were human — Wells's Martians injected human blood directly into their veins and were sustained by it. Cross-species blood transfusion causes fatal hemolytic reactions due to antigen incompatibility — yet the blood nourished them. Only the bacteria killed them. Antigen compatibility at this level is only possible within the same species. Wells never describes them feeding on any non-human terrestrial animal. The bipedal creatures brought from Mars were food provisions (eaten, not used for transfusion) — the blood-injection protocol was used exclusively with human victims.

For detailed analysis: The Deep State is on Mars

Brandenburg's Death on Mars (2015) dates Mars devastation at ~180 Ma — managed disclosure dead end; see 12.06.01 Brandenburg's Nuclear Evidence.

The Carbon Dating Problem

If Mars (and portions of Earth) were recently incinerated — within the last thousand years — why does radiometric dating show "billions of years"? Because carbon dating cannot record a massive recent energy spike as recent. A catastrophic event incinerating all organic material resets the carbon clock to zero: no living carbon remains to begin the decay sequence. What survives is mineral carbon — locked in rock and glass — which reads as "very old" by default. Mainstream science concludes both planets are billions of years old, when the surfaces we photograph may have been melted within recorded human memory. The question is not whether the damage is ancient or recent — it is whether the dating method can distinguish the two.

The American Revolution

The American Revolution (1775–1783) was not an isolated colonial revolt. It was a small piece of a much larger Atlantic realignment — one node in a network of British debt, French intervention, Dutch finance, and the final fracturing of the Hordian remnants in North America.

The Eastern Front of the Pugachev War

Fomenko's New Chronology identifies the American War of Independence as the eastern front of the same conflict that produced the Pugachev Rebellion:

"'The War of independence' in North America was the struggle with the weakening Russian Horde. The Romanovs attacked the Horde from the West — and from the East in America it was attacked by the Americans 'fighting for independence'."
— Fomenko, History: Fiction or Science? Ch.8 §1

Pugachev's defeat (1774–1775) coincided with — and enabled — the American colonies' seizure of Hordian territory in the continent. The "thirteen colonies" were the eastern theatre; Moscow Tartary's collapse was the western.

The Bank of England Connection

The revolution cannot be understood without the London Financial Coup (1664–1694). The Bank of England and its debt architecture required colonial taxation to service. The entire sequence — Stamp Act (1765), Townshend Acts, Tea Act — was debt extraction by a financial system barely seventy years old. The colonists were not rejecting "tyranny" in the abstract; they were resisting a specific financial apparatus.

France bankrolled the American war with loans and military support, which directly triggered the French fiscal crisis that became the French Revolution (1789). The two revolutions are linked by capital flows — and by the same pattern of societized consolidation documented in the British Civil War investigation.

What the Revolution Did Not Achieve

• Slavery: Preserved and constitutionalized

• Indigenous sovereignty: Accelerated westward expansion; British treaties (Proclamation of 1763) voided

• Financial independence: The new republic was immediately indebted to French and Dutch creditors; Hamilton's assumption of state debts (1790) created a national debt structure modeled on Britain's

The revolution replaced one debt master with another. The Bank of England's template — central bank, national debt, parliamentary management — was reproduced by Hamilton within seven years of independence.

See: American Revolution Investigation for full evidence and Atlantic context.

The French Revolution

The French Revolution (1789–1799) — like the British Civil War before it — predated the country's major imperial expansion and provided the institutional groundwork for it. The revolution accomplished a societized consolidation of resources and political reorganization that replaced divine-right monarchy with the template for modern Western governance.

The Pattern: Revolution Before Empire

Country
Revolution
Imperial Expansion

Britain
Civil War (1642–1651); Glorious Revolution (1688)
British Empire peak (18th–19th c.)

France
French Revolution (1789–1799)
Napoleonic Empire; later colonial empire

In both cases, the revolution reorganized the state before imperial expansion: centralized taxation, standardized administration, meritocratic military, and ideological mobilization.

Societized Consolidation

The Revolution transferred resources from Crown, Church, and nobility to institutions claiming to represent "the nation":

• Church lands nationalized (November 1789) — sold to create loyal proprietors

• Feudalism abolished (August 4, 1789) — seigneurial dues, tithes, privileges ended

• Levée en masse (1793) — total mobilization; citizens became soldiers

This is the same process the British Civil War pioneered: Crown/Church assets redirected to Parliament's merchant-finance coalition.

The Four Pillars of Western Governance

The Revolution produced the political template the West still uses:

• Meritocracy — La carrière ouverte aux talents. Napoleon rose from artillery officer to Emperor. Competence replaced birthright.

• Plutocracy — Property qualifications for voting (Constitution of 1791). Wealth as gate to power.

• Particracy — Rule by faction: Jacobins, Girondins, Thermidorians. Party structure as governance.

• Electoral Autocracy — Strong executive, weak legislature, popular mandate via plebiscite. Napoleon's coups ratified by referendum.

The Direct Line to 1812

The Revolution produced the conditions for Napoleon's 1812 invasion of Russia — the first Reverse Crusade. A centralized, ideologically motivated state with mass conscription and meritocratic officers was the prerequisite for marching an army east. Without the Revolution, there is no Napoleonic Wars.

Trail of Tears: Century-Long Erasure

The Trail of Tears is remembered as a forced relocation of Native Americans in the 1830s. But the real story begins eighty-one years earlier, in 1758, with the establishment of the first Indian reservation in North America — the Brotherton Indian Reservation (Edgepillock) in New Jersey.

This predates the MudFlood Energetic Event (~1774) by sixteen years. The reservations did not follow the MFEE — they preceded it. What followed was not a single march, but a century-long campaign to erase Native American civilization, memory, and technology.

The Timeline: 1758 to 1851

1758: The First Reservation — Before the MFEE

The Brotherton Indian Reservation was established in Burlington County, New Jersey, in 1758 — the first formal reservation in North America. At this time, Native Americans still possessed:

• Advanced agricultural technology

• Sophisticated governance systems (Iroquois Confederacy influenced the U.S. Constitution)

• Complex trade networks spanning the continent

• Knowledge of metallurgy, earthworks, and large-scale construction

The reservation system began the process of confinement — forcing indigenous peoples onto restricted lands, severing trade routes, isolating communities.

1774: The MudFlood Energetic Event

Sixteen years after the first reservation, the MudFlood Energetic Event devastates the planet. Cities are buried, populations vanish, entire regions are obliterated. The Hordian world — including remnants of the Rus-Horde Empire in North America — is reduced to wreckage.

Native Americans, already confined to reservations, are now surrounded by a depopulated landscape. The survivors of the MFEE — those who did not perish in the cataclysm — are scattered, their infrastructure destroyed, their knowledge fragmented.

1830–1839: The Official "Trail of Tears"

By 1830, the U.S. government formalizes what had been an ongoing process for seventy-two years. The Indian Removal Act of 1830 mandates the relocation of the Five Civilized Tribes (Cherokee, Muscogee, Seminole, Chickasaw, Choctaw) to lands west of the Mississippi.

Between 1830 and 1839, an estimated 60,000–100,000 Native Americans are forcibly marched across ~5,045 miles. Death toll: 13,200–16,700 (official estimate; likely higher).

But this is not the beginning. It is the end.

By 1839, Native Americans have been confined, displaced, starved, and stripped of their technology for eighty-one years. The 1830s relocation is the final stage of a process that began in 1758.

1851: Indian Appropriations Act — Institutionalizing the System

The Indian Appropriations Act of 1851 officially initiates the federal reservation system, consolidating what had been an ad-hoc policy for nearly a century. By this point:

• Native Americans have lost their kingdoms

• Advanced technology is gone (or hidden/suppressed)

• The survivors are using "sticks and stones" — the image we inherit

• 408 Indian boarding schools (1819–1969) enforce cultural erasure: "Kill the Indian, save the man"

Total duration: At least 93 years (1758–1851), arguably 211 (1758–1969). By 1839 the transformation was complete: Native Americans had lost their kingdoms, technology, and autonomy — confined to reservations, subjected to boarding schools, reduced to the "sticks and stones" narrative imposed by the erasure campaign.

The Bering–Siberia Thesis (Speculative)

The strongest, most resistant populations may have been driven far beyond Oklahoma. Fomenko notes that Siberia became an exile destination after Pugachev (1774). Timing suggests:

• Before 1774: Romanovs could not exile to Siberia — it was Moscow Tartary (Horde heartland)

• After 1774: Siberia is "wreckage" — depopulated, available as penal colony

• After 1790: First documented exile to Tobolsk (Radishev, 1790)

Theory: The most resistant Native American populations may have been driven across the Bering Strait into Siberia. Weaker groups remained on U.S. reservations. No primary-source evidence; timing suggestive.

Mesoamerican Genocide — Same Campaign

The destruction of Maya and Aztec civilization is part of the same broad genocide. Mainstream history dates the "Maya collapse" to 800–900 CE — pushing recent crimes into the distant past. The 1492 CE Apocalypse Crusade extended into the Americas; what mainstream records as "Aztec genocide of the Mayans" or Spanish conquest was the Crusade's advance. The 18th–19th century campaign documented here (1758–1839+) completed the erasure of indigenous peoples across North America — including remnants of Mesoamerican civilization. See Common Questions: Why did the Maya civilization collapse? for the redacted-history analysis.

Cross-References

13.00.00-19th-century-the-rise-of-communism.md

19th Century: The Rise of Communism

The 19th century is the century in which the remnants of the old Rus-Horde finally lost their grip on Eurasia. After the crushing of Pugachev's rebellion (1773–75) and Reverse Crusade I (the Napoleonic wars — the first pan-European invasion of Russia), the former world-empire was carved into competing nation-states, each with a manufactured "ancient" history and a newly constructed national language. But the Romanov usurpers who held Russia still faced a problem: the population remembered. The Cossack traditions, the old communal land systems, the village communes (obshchina) — all of these carried the living memory of how the Horde had actually governed: collectively, with shared resources and obligations.

Into this volatile 19th-century landscape stepped a new weapon: ideology as social engineering. The Pyramidal Empire operators behind the fragmentation of the old Empire needed to channel the natural communal instincts of the Russian and European peoples — instincts that were a direct inheritance from the Hordian system — into a form they could control. They needed a version of communism that would not restore the old order but destroy it utterly before replacing it with something manageable.

This is the context in which Karl Marx appeared.

A Note on Sources: The Most Redacted Centuries

Fomenko himself observes that primary sources from the late 18th, 19th, and 20th centuries are disproportionately compromised — hidden, altered, or removed from academic view. His own corpus draws more heavily on older documents than on modern ones. This is counterintuitive: one would expect the most recent centuries to be the best documented. The opposite is the case.

The reason is straightforward. Events like the MudFlood, the destruction of the Hordian Empire, and the wholesale rewriting of world chronology occurred within living memory of the 18th and 19th centuries. Covering up a global cataclysm that survivors could still describe required the most aggressive redaction campaign in history — far more thorough than redacting events from centuries earlier, where fewer witnesses remained. The closer the event, the harder it is to hide, and the more evidence must be destroyed.

From this chapter onward, the timeline relies more heavily on the author's own analysis and political speculation. This is not a weakness — it is a methodological necessity. The earlier chapters rest on Fomenko's statistical chronology, Saturnian cosmology's plasma physics, and cross-cultural mythology. Those frameworks provide the context — the causal chain — that makes it possible to interpret the heavily redacted modern record. The timeline fills in what the sources cannot, allowing us to speculate on these centuries with enough structural context to reach conclusions that can predict future events accurately. Where the reader encounters political analysis in Chapters 13–15, understand that this is the author applying the timeline's causal logic to a period where conventional sources have been systematically stripped.

The Century of Manufactured Prophets

The 19th century was marked by an explosion of mass-published political theorists whose works were distributed at a scale and speed that no organic intellectual movement could have achieved. Marx's Das Kapital and The Communist Manifesto (co-authored with Engels) were published, translated, and disseminated across Europe with a logistical efficiency that suggests powerful backing — not the grassroots spread of dangerous ideas that the official narrative describes.

Meanwhile, genuinely independent thinkers who proposed alternative models of social organisation — models that preserved spiritual, psychological, and cultural dimensions alongside the economic — were systematically marginalised, suppressed, or simply never given access to the same publication networks.

This chapter covers Marx's manufacture as controlled-opposition prophet, the stripping of spirituality from communism into a materialist death-cult, Kinkel's superior alternative to Marxism, and the suppression of independent thinkers — the long game by which the "prophecy" of violent revolution was planted decades before 1917.

13.01.00-napoleonic-wars-reverse.md

Napoleonic Wars — Reverse Crusades

The Napoleonic Wars (1803–1815) were the final major armed conflict of the post-Hordian transition — the last attempt to create a unified continental European power resisting the City of London's financial empire. Napoleon's campaigns swept across old Imperial territories, dismantling feudal aristocracy and replacing it with the Napoleonic Code — rationalized law based on property rights, secular governance, and civil equality.

In the Fomenko framework, the original Crusades were launched by Russia — the Horde expanding west. The Cathars were already destroyed in a "reversed Holy Crusade."

1812 CE — Napoleon invades Russia — Reverse Crusade I

The Napoleonic invasion of 1812 was the first full-scale reversal: all of Europe marching into Russia. We designate this Reverse Crusade I — the first of four. Napoleon's Grande Armée's catastrophic six-month campaign — Borodino (7 Sept 1812), Moscow burned by its defenders, the winter retreat — was directed by the same financial network (City of London) whose target was always Russia. See Napoleon 1812 Campaign: Extended and Aftermath of 1812 for the full narrative. For artillery, balloons, and suppressed-tech hypotheses — sound / resonant and lighter-than-air only; not plasma cannons — see /governance/war/investigations/napoleonic-advanced-artillery-airship-investigation.md.

Weather and post-MFEE ecology (possible factor): Staff work and folk meteorology in France could have looked reliable in a theatre where the MFEE and mud-flood aftereffects registered as comparatively mild — Western Europe’s climate and hydrology had not, on this reading, been wrenched as far off baseline as belts farther east. Siberia and the inner-continental Russian northeast, by contrast, may have carried heavier ecological damage and therefore sharper, less predictable swings (drought, early freeze, thaw-mud cycles) than models tuned on French seasons could anticipate. French command may have assumed the campaign could read sky and calendar the way it did in Italy or Germany, without accounting for a harsher post-cataclysm imbalance in the east. That is suggestive only: it would amplify, not replace, scorched earth, distance, and coalition strategy as explanations for the winter disaster.

Napoleon and the Financial Revolution

Napoleon's relationship to Europe's financial restructuring was paradoxical. He abolished the Inquisition in Venice in 1806, liberated Jewish populations, and promoted their legal equality. Gueniffey: Napoleon promoted Jewish assimilation into the French nation. His central bank project, however, competed directly with the Bank of England's debt-based system. His Continental System — an economic blockade of British goods — aimed to destroy the City of London's financial dominance. This is the real reason Britain never stopped fighting him. The Napoleonic Wars were a war between two financial systems: property-based order versus debt-based empire.

The Napoleon–Britain Paradox

If Napoleon was genuinely at war with Britain, his actions make no sense. In 1803 he sold Louisiana — the heartland of North America — to Britain's own American colony for a fraction of its value. He assembled a "Grand Army of England" on the Channel coast for two years (1803–1805) and never launched it. The Battle of Trafalgar (1805) is offered as the explanation — but if a united continent truly sought to invade England, there were a hundred miles of coastline and no permanent fortification. Naval superiority does not explain why Napoleon never attempted a crossing and then chose to march 2,000 miles east into Russia instead.

Hitler repeated the identical pattern in 1940–41: he assembled Operation Sea Lion, postponed it indefinitely, and turned the entire Wehrmacht east into Russia — Reverse Crusade III. Historians must invoke "hubris" or "miscalculation" twice, for two different men in two different centuries, to explain the same inexplicable strategic decision. The simpler answer: England was never the real target because England housed the command structure. Both Napoleon and Hitler were directed by the financial networks centered on the City of London. Their assignment was always the same — destroy the last successor of the Horde.

Revolutionary Prelude (1792–1797): France's transition from monarchy to financial governance; Jacobins = same class that transformed England. Egyptian Campaign (1798–1801): Rosetta Stone, Description de l'Égypte — NC: ruins far more recent; Hordian cemetery system. For timeline: Napoleonic Extended Evidence.

The Continental and Anti-Continental Coalitions

France faced seven successive coalitions organized by Britain and financed by the City of London. Each was broken militarily by Napoleon; each was reassembled through new British subsidies. The pattern reveals the asymmetry: Napoleon could win every battle and still lose, because his enemy's primary weapon was credit, not armies.

The Romanov-British partnership is critical context: the dynasty that partitioned Moscow Tartary now served as the eastern arm of British containment. The 1812 Russian campaign resulted not from military genius but deliberate scorched-earth strategy combined with climate — and the impossibility of occupying Moscow without triggering full Romanov-British resource mobilization.

For the military aftermath — Russian pursuit to Paris, the occupation of France, and the cultural collision — see Aftermath of 1812.

Napoleon as Puppet: The Foundation of Managed Democracy

The conventional image of Napoleon as a self-made genius who seized power through sheer will does not survive scrutiny. Napoleon took orders his entire career — even when it appeared otherwise. He was elevated, directed, and ultimately discarded by the same financial networks that organised the coalitions against him. His role was to destroy the feudal order from within, replacing it with a rationalised legal-financial framework compatible with debt-based governance. When that task was complete, he was removed.

This pattern — the charismatic "strongman" who appears to act independently but serves a hidden principal — is the prototype of puppet democracy, the operational foundation of Marxism as actually applied. Hitler repeated the pattern: a seemingly independent nationalist leader whose every strategic decision served the interests of the financial powers he claimed to oppose. Zelensky continues it today. In each case, the puppet is expendable; the system behind the puppet is not.

Napoleon did not have a "Napoleonic complex." He had handlers. The so-called complex was invented to make the puppet seem self-directed — the same mechanism that attributes Hitler's decisions to madness rather than instruction, or Zelensky's to patriotism rather than obligation.

Tech ceiling, sabotage, and the Russian outcome

On this reading, the Grande Armée was only as technically empowered as its creators needed it to be: enough to wound Russia and force continental reordering, not enough to consolidate a self-sufficient Eurasian empire that could defy the City of London and allied command. Plasma cannons are ruled out for this era — they exceed credible public science of the time and do not fit an army still partly composed of revolutionary levies with fresh memory of toppling throne and altar; military science was already obscured, compartmentalized, and withheld from both mass soldiery and any figurehead who might turn. What may have been selectively released aligns with graded chemical artillery, acoustic or resonant devices, and balloon or dirigible lineage — not discharge weapons that would require a priestly technical caste on campaign.

The same pattern runs across Reverse Crusades (Napoleon, Hitler, Zelensky): each advanced Western or proxy force lost or stalled against Russia despite paper superiority because the networks that built the figurehead could not permit a victory so total that the puppet regime escaped dependency. Sabotage — logistics, intelligence, incomplete briefings, withheld capabilities — pairs with misplaced faith in opaque institutions. Russia prevailed through depth, home defence, scorched earth, and an enemy whose real chain of command never intended an uncontrollable winner. See Reverse Crusades — structural comparison.

The Aftermath: Restored Order and Managed States

The Congress of Vienna (1814–1815) restored European monarchies with one crucial difference: every restored monarchy now operated within a framework of national debt to international banking houses (principally the Rothschilds, who had funded multiple sides and emerged as dominant European financial power). Political independence was real; financial independence was not. The post-Napoleonic order was the Westphalian system completed: sovereign states whose sovereignty operated within boundaries set by their creditors.

Aftermath of 1812: Russian Pursuit to Paris

Following Napoleon's catastrophic retreat from Moscow, the Russian army did not stop at the border. Under Czar Alexander I, Russian forces pursued the remnants of the Grande Armée westward across Europe, reaching Paris itself in March 1814. Russian troops occupied parts of France — Cossack cavalry camped on the Champs-Élysées. The occupation lasted until 1818 under the terms of the Congress of Vienna. It was the farthest west a Russian army would march until 1945. For the Reverse Crusade thesis and Horde/Crusades context, see Napoleonic Wars.

Small Humans, Advanced Technology

Pugachev was the last war between giant-lineage sovereigns and smaller western humans with superior technology. Napoleonic Wars = continuation. Napoleon ~4'; western armies had technology generations ahead — electromagnetic/plasma systems inherited from Mars-firing civilization. Devastating in temperate conditions; never tested against Russian winter. For Russian Mountains (roller coasters), Napoleon height detail: Napoleonic Extended Evidence.

Why the Winter Won

The French failure in Russia is conventionally attributed to hubris and logistical overreach. Two deeper factors were at work:

•
Unpredictable weather after the MFEE. The MudFlood Energetic Event of ~1774 had fundamentally destabilized Earth's climate. Weather patterns that had been predictable for millennia were now chaotic. The French high command planned a summer campaign based on historical climate data that was no longer valid. The early onset of the 1812 winter caught them unprepared not because they were careless but because the old atmospheric models had been shattered by the MFEE barely forty years prior.

•
Advanced technology failed in extreme cold. Whatever electromagnetic or plasma-based weapons and transport the French possessed, these systems operated within temperature and atmospheric parameters that a Russian winter exceeded. Floating airships grounded. Energy weapons misfired or failed to charge. The technological superiority that had swept every European army aside became useless in conditions the operators had never encountered and could not adapt to.

The Temple They Burned Themselves

When the Russians burned Moscow before Napoleon's entry, the destruction included the city's most sacred structures. The population preferred to destroy their own holy places — fearing the French would convert them into brothels or worse — rather than see them desecrated by an occupying force that had no understanding of or respect for Orthodox Hordian tradition. This act demonstrates what the locals understood about their invaders: this was not a liberating army. It was a hostile civilization.

A population that burns its own Jerusalem will never be governed by the force that drove them to it. Napoleon occupied ashes for five weeks, found no allies, no resources, and no submission. The strategy was doomed — as it was doomed when Hitler repeated it in Reverse Crusade III (1941), and as it continues to fail in Reverse Crusade IV today. The land rejects the occupier because the people reject the system the occupier serves.

Russian Mountains: French encountered elaborate ice slides (20–25m ramps); astonished. Paris engineers built wheeled versions — origin of roller coasters; still called Montagnes Russes in French/Spanish/Italian. See Napoleonic Extended Evidence.

13.01.03-1840-ce-ecliptic-pathway-of-the-absu-last-seen.md

Ecliptic pathway of the Absu last seen.

The Absu was Saturn's primordial plasma sheath — equatorial ring-layers enveloping Earth and the inner planets during the Golden Age. After the polar configuration collapsed in 2349 BC (Noah's Flood), the inner Absu fell — but an outer ring persisted.

The Last Ring: The Uoroboros

One outer ring survived 2349 BC. Red, sitting ~6,000 miles above Earth's equator, Earth's shadow made it appear each night as a glowing snake whose head rose in the east and folded back on its own tail — the Uoroboros. The Egyptians called it Apep, Greeks Apophis, Hindus Vritra, Babylonians Tiamat, Australians the Rainbow Serpent. Same red equatorial ring, observed from every continent.

The ecliptic crossed this ring at equinoxes, forming a giant "X" — the Maya Saint Andrew's Cross hieroglyph. By AD 600–800 the ring faded; by ~AD 900 it disappeared entirely.

Absu Dust: 4,000 Years of Falling Particles

Even after the rings dissolved, remnant charged particles continued raining down for millennia. Charles Raspil ("Spatters and Planetary Iconography," The Velikovskian, 1994) documented characteristic spatter patterns in artwork from the mid-second millennium BC through the 18th century AD — Rhodian plates, Near Eastern reliefs, Egyptian papyri, Mesoamerican codices, Renaissance paintings. These appear as background texture, not symbols — objects actually seen.

Terminal Dates

Cook records two reference points for the end:

• ~AD 1600 — "Last of the falling Absu sprinkles"

• ~AD 1700 — "Last sprinkling of Absu dust"

The Ecliptic Pathway: Last Seen AD 1840

The ecliptic pathway — zodiacal material strewn along the orbital plane by Jupiter's plasma bolts in the asteroid belt — remained faintly visible for another century.

"The destruction wrought by Jupiter with plasma bolts in the asteroid belt would have left dust and debris strewn along the ecliptic, causing the 'path of the Gods' to be marked like a highway. The lighted zodiac was last seen from Earth in AD 1840."
— Jno Cook, SaturnianCosmology.org

The complete terminal sequence:

• ~AD 600–800: Red Uoroboros ring fades

• ~AD 900: Uoroboros fully gone

• ~AD 1600–1700: Last Absu dust reaches ground

• AD 1840: Ecliptic zodiacal pathway last observed

Connection to the MudFlood Event

The convergence with the late 18th-century political crisis is striking. The Pugachev Rebellion erupted in 1773 — within decades of the last Absu dust and the final ecliptic fading. The Absu's long dispersal maintained a persistent electrical connection between Earth's atmosphere and remnant Saturn-system material. As this cleared, atmospheric equilibrium broke. The MudFlood Event may reflect a catastrophic discharge event made possible once the protective Absu charge envelope was gone.

13.01.04-1880-ce-tchaikovsky-releases-1812-overture.md

Tchaikovsky releases 1812 Overture

Pyotr Ilyich Tchaikovsky composed the 1812 Overture in 1880, commissioned for the consecration of the Cathedral of Christ the Saviour in Moscow. Its premiere featured live cannon fire, church bells, and massed military bands.

Tchaikovsky Hated It

Tchaikovsky privately despised the 1812 Overture. In a letter to his patroness Nadezhda von Meck, he wrote:

"The overture will be very loud and noisy, but I wrote it with no warm feeling of love, and therefore there will probably be no artistic merits in it."

He later called it "not at all good" and expressed embarrassment at its popularity. This raises a question the conventional narrative cannot answer: why would a composer of Tchaikovsky's stature accept a commission he found artistically repugnant?

The theory: Tchaikovsky was ordered to write it. By 1880, France was under the Third Republic, successor to the Second French Empire of Napoleon III. The Franco-Russian alliance was being carefully cultivated — it would be formalised in 1894. Leaders aligned with French interests needed a grand public narrative of Russian-French reconciliation: a musical monument to 1812 that framed the war as a completed chapter rather than an ongoing civilizational conflict. Tchaikovsky, dependent on state patronage, had no ability to refuse. He did the minimum: produced a technically competent crowd-pleaser with no personal investment, and said so privately.

The Orthodox Hymn vs. the Marseillaise

The overture opens with the Russian Orthodox hymn God Preserve Thy People, drawn from the ancient Kievan liturgical tradition — the music of the old Hordian spiritual world. This is pitted against the Marseillaise (French Revolutionary anthem) and overwhelmed by cannon blasts and bells. The thematic contest is between the old Orthodox-Imperial order and the new Financial-Republican order Napoleon carried across Europe.

For the financial and military context of the Napoleonic Wars — the coalitions, the Continental System, Napoleon's role as puppet, the Napoleon–Britain paradox, and the 1812 invasion itself — see Napoleonic Wars — Reverse Crusade I and Napoleon invades Russia.

The Jesuit Jerusalem Project

Parallel to the Napoleonic wars, the Jesuits completed their chronological project: marching pilgrims past actual ruins in Turkey (Constantinople/Czar-Grad = Biblical Jerusalem) and founding Jerusalem III at Al-Aqsa Mosque in Palestine. See The Jesuit Restoration.

Tchaikovsky's Musical Testament

Tchaikovsky composed the overture sixty-eight years after the event. By 1880, the old Hordian world was visible only in the Orthodox Church's liturgical music — the same music opening his overture. The cannon fire ending it represents both the Russian victory of 1812 and the gunpowder used to demolish Cathar castles, Hordian fortifications, and every state refusing the new financial order. In the NC reading, the 1812 Overture is the last great piece of counter-historical art: a musical argument that the old world was worth fighting for. By the time Tchaikovsky wrote it, that argument had already been lost.

Battle for Communism's Soul

There Was Never One Communism

The 19th century produced not one "communism" but a war between radically different visions. Marx's version won — not on merit, but through mass-distribution via controlled publication networks while alternatives reached very few.

All communisms head down one of two roads: dictatorship/fascism (vanguard becomes the new ruling class) or republicanism (revolutionary energy matures into self-governance). Most 19th-century socialists reached for the second road. Marx's version was designed to make that transition impossible.

Who Funded the Printing Press?

Friedrich Engels — a capitalist's son — was Marx's lifelong patron. Marx lived in London, heart of the world's largest empire, never meaningfully suppressed. Communist Manifesto (1848) published simultaneously in multiple languages. Gottfried Kinkel's Bonner Zeitung reached a handful; Ivan Kinkel's 312-page theory (1921) was never translated from Bulgarian. The Pyramidal Empire didn't silence alternatives — they made Marx louder by orders of magnitude.

Marx's Demolition Manual

Stripped to essentials: (1) do not reform; (2) violent revolution is inevitable; (3) everything old must be destroyed; (4) only after total destruction can new society emerge. This ensured nothing could be reconstructed — certainly not a republic.

Ivan Kinkel demonstrated Marx's economic determinism is absurdly reductive — a "universal phlogiston," invoked to explain everything while explaining nothing. (See The Hijacking of Communism.)

The operators behind Rus-Horde fragmentation needed exactly this: a prophecy convincing Russians their communal Hordian memory was worthless "primitive communism" and spiritual traditions were opium. The 1917 revolution fulfilled Marx's script — old Russia, with all its Hordian memory, was incinerated.

Gottfried Kinkel: The Road Not Taken

Johann Gottfried Kinkel (1815–1882) — poet, art historian, Bonn professor — unlike Marx, actually fought in 1848. Wounded, arrested, sentenced to life. Carl Schurz broke him out of Spandau; Dickens campaigned for his release. Schurz became U.S. Senator — showing what 1848 revolutionaries could have become.

Kinkel opposed Marx on the fundamental question: act now, or wait for "conditions to ripen"? Kinkel said: build republic through courage and democratic action. Marx despised this. Kinkel's wife Johanna — composer, journalist, revolutionary — fell or threw herself from a window in London, 1858. Tombstone: Freiheit, Liebe und Dichtung — "Freedom, Love, and Poetry."

The Pyramidal Empire shaped communism like bonsai: carefully pruned, deliberately stunted. Marx provided wire and shears — ensuring revolution would be violent, require a vanguard party (ready-made for infiltration), abolish all traditions, and produce state capitalism administered by party aristocracy.

13.02.01-1883-ce-the-hijacking-of-communism.md
